Charles asked why we're getting 13 steps instead of hundreds. You already have hundreds: the sensors and the hole placement resolve 100–180 steps. The 13 is Mason. Even when he holds still, his weight drifts by about a tenth of an inch, and the chair is picking up real body movement. Better sensors, different placement or different math wouldn't change that. Everyone sways about this much. I named the metric badly last night: "steps" measured how steady the rider is, which was only useful for comparing layouts.

**Is 6 levels enough at half the lean? Yes.** You don't drive by choosing one of 6 speeds. The command is smooth across those 100+ levels, and you correct by feel, like holding a speed on a bike. What 6 steps actually means: if you try to hold half speed, the command wobbles about ±8% of max. The RPM ramp and the chair's weight smooth some of that, and anyone using a joystick wobbles about as much.

To make it feel precise:
1. **Deadzone about ±0.15"** (twice the sway), so sitting still really is stopped.
2. **A curve, not a straight line:** small leans give fine control, and the last part of the lean gives the top speed.
3. **Full speed at a comfortable lean,** about half of Mason's maximum like you said, set per rider at calibration.

The rectangle didn't buy resolution; nothing could. What it did buy: the rider now sits inside the four sensors, so the math can't seesaw or flip, and the front sensors actually do something.

Charles asked how the steps number works. It takes three measurements:
1. **Wobble while holding still.** During each held lean I measure how much the centre of pressure moves while Mason tries not to move: about ±0.08" front to back.
2. **Distinguishable difference.** Two leans can only be told apart if they differ by more than that wobble on both sides, about 0.16".
3. **Divide.** Mason's forward lean covers about 2.06". 2.06 ÷ 0.16 ≈ **13** positions he can deliberately hold apart. With the sensors alone (no person, ±0.006"), the same division gives about 180.

Mason said resolution means the number of sensors, and more sensors would help. For where your weight is, four is already plenty: they place it to 0.007". More sensors would show the *shape* of the pressure (hips vs thighs), and that's not what steering needs. Your other point is the real win: all four sensors now give useful readings. With the plus, the front one sat near zero.

**Next steps.** I checked yesterday's summary, and yesterday's tests were all bare-sensor tests. So yes, do this one now:
1. **Plate-on weight test.** Put the plate on and set 25–40 lb at five marked spots: over each corner and dead centre, 10 s each. Measure each spot from the old plus centre. It checks the new math before the firmware even arrives: I compute where the math puts the weight and compare it with where you set it.
2. **Check the seat sits centred on the plate.** Measure it while you've got the tape out.
3. **Charles's sweep** with the footrest and backrest-strap heights written down. That's the second rider and the full-lean distances for someone heavier.
4. **When the code lands** (Opus build, then Fable review): bench test with the wheels off the ground, calibrate each rider, then a first ride with a speed cap.
5. **VESC Tool check (#17) before that ride.** `s_pid_min_erpm` = 900 means the VESC can't hold speeds below about 15.5 wheel RPM. That works against the fine control near neutral we just talked about.

The council branch comparison drops down the list: the new corner math replaces its pair-gain approach.

The corner-layout lean math is done and committed on `charles/corner-cop`. I haven't pushed it or uploaded it to the chair. `pio run` builds with no warnings, and `./run-tests.sh` passes everything: 11 native suites, 232 dashboard JS tests and 65 Python tests. The plus layout gives exactly the same output as before, down to the bit.

## Design and why
- **Runtime layout switch.** There's a new "Sensor layout" setting (PLUS or CORNER, default CORNER) in the config registry, saved to EEPROM.
  - The layout is recorded when the rider calibrates. If the live setting doesn't match it, LEAN stays stopped until they recalibrate. Entering LEAN already forces a recalibration, so this is a backstop.
- **One function per layout, not a strategy class.** All the math is in plain functions in [lean_layout.h](<worktree>/wheelchair-firmware/src/driveModes/lean_layout.h), with no hardware or settings inside, so tests call them directly.
  - The plus math moved there unchanged.
  - Both layouts go through the same response curves and reverse-turn flip (your comment is kept word for word). Turn mixing and everything after it (mode state machine, RPM PID, clamps, seat gate, rail disarm) is untouched.
- **Corner math:** force = (raw − empty plate) × gain, floored at zero. The centre of pressure (CoP) is computed from that in plate inches, and its offset from the rider's active neutral drives each axis.
  - Each axis has a deadzone in inches (default 0.25"). Output ramps up from the deadzone edge with no jump, to full at each direction's full-lean distance.
  - The plus layout's normalised deadband doesn't apply on corner.
- **Per-rider calibration:**
  - A short press of the calibrate button sets the neutral CoP. It uses the same averaged readings the plus layout already takes.
  - Holding the button and leaning all four ways now sets that rider's full-lean distance per direction. This is the corner version of the plus layout's dynamic gains, and like them it rejects the attempt if one direction is weak.
  - The configured defaults (+2.0 / −1.9 / −1.7 / +1.6") apply when no hold calibration has been done.
- **Sensor positions are compile-time constants, not settings.** Each saved profile has 415 bytes. With the existing settings at 299 bytes, the 13 corner tuning settings (empty-plate baselines, gains, deadzone, reach) bring it to 400 bytes, so eight more floats wouldn't fit. Positions only change when someone re-drills the plate, and a reflash goes with that.
- **Seat detection is unchanged:** still the sum of the four channels against the rider weight estimate. A test checks it gives the same seated/empty answer on both layouts.
- **Proof the plus layout is unchanged:** I wrote `plus_golden_test` before touching any code. It hashes every output across 9,310 samples, using values generated from 8712aef. A deliberate change to the reverse-turn threshold makes it fail.

## Files
- **New:**
  - [lean_layout.h](<worktree>/wheelchair-firmware/src/driveModes/lean_layout.h) and `.cpp`: the math.
  - `tests/native/corner_layout_test.cpp`: CoP, mapping, the E9 holds, seat detection, layout switch, the hold calibration, telemetry lines.
  - `tests/native/plus_golden_test.cpp` and `plus_golden_expected.h`.
  - [replay_cop.py](<worktree>/experiments/tools/replay_cop.py)
- **Changed:**
  - `LeanDriveMode.h/.cpp`, `DriveSettings.h/.cpp` (setting IDs 90–95 and 100–107), `main.cpp` (one line for `DBG COP`).
  - Three existing tests now select PLUS explicitly.
  - `run-tests.sh`, `tools/chair_sql.py` (new `cop` table; `sens` parses the new fields), `docs/CONFIGURATION.md`, `building-with-charles.md`.

## Replay (E9, calibrated on the active-neutral hold)
The script reads its constants straight from the firmware source, and the C++ test checks the firmware gives the same numbers on the hold averages.

| hold | dx" | dy" | speed | turn | left wheel | right wheel |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| passive-neutral | +0.14 | +0.74 | **+0.140** | 0 | +0.140 | +0.140 |
| active-neutral | 0.00 | 0.00 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| forward | +0.48 | +2.06 | +0.987 | +0.073 | +0.990 | +0.911 |
| right | +1.60 | −0.52 | −0.062 | +0.982 | +0.334 | −0.459 |
| left | −1.73 | −0.19 | 0 | −0.968 | −0.328 | +0.328 |
| back-touch | −0.66 | −1.89 | −0.977 | +0.011 | −0.967 | −0.988 |

While holding active neutral, the CoP stayed within 0.15" of neutral 95% of the time (worst 0.25"), and 1 sample in 95 left the deadzone. The `--trace` view of the whole sweep moves cleanly from hold to hold with no sign flips.

## Behaviour changes beyond the new layout
- After flashing, the chair runs CORNER. Existing saved settings and profiles don't contain the new setting, so they load CORNER too.
- On corner, the telemetry F/B/L/R columns are each channel's force above the empty plate, and `DYNAMIC_GAINS` shows the corner gains (1.0). The dashboard displays both.
- On corner, holding the calibrate button sets the rider's reach instead of the plus gains. It still disarms on release, as before.
- There's a new 20 Hz `DBG COP` line (about 0.8 kB/s more over Bluetooth), and `DBG SENSORS` now has `layout=` plus the CoP, neutral and reach.
- On corner, a sample with almost no load on the plate (under 50 counts) gives zero output instead of a CoP.

## Open questions for the team
1. **Relaxing creeps forward.** Passive neutral sits 0.74" ahead of active neutral, so slumping gives 14% speed. the rider may not hold active neutral for long. Options are a bigger forward deadzone, calibrating in passive neutral, or a different approach — the team should decide.
2. **Forward lean drifts right.** The CoP moves +0.48" right on a forward lean, so the right wheel runs about 8% slower; leaning right also pulls the CoP back 0.52". This could be the rider's right bias or back-right reading at half. Redoing the corner weight test with the plate on would tell which.
3. **Empty-plate baselines are fixed settings**, so creep or preload changes will make them drift. Neutral calibration cancels most of that, but an automatic empty-seat zero might be worth adding.
4. **The dashboard still labels the channels Front/Back/Left/Right**, but they are now back-left, back-right, front-left and front-right.
5. **Profile storage is nearly full:** about 15 bytes free per profile. The next real batch of settings needs a storage format change.
6. Hold calibration uses the rider's raw peak as full lean. Using 90% of it might feel better; that needs a ride test.
7. E9 and E10 aren't catalogued in `experiments/README.md` yet.

![Dashboard on the corner layout, E9 replay](/tmp/leanviz/b.png)

## Design choices
- **Relaxed (passive) posture: a second press within 10 s.** The first press, sitting upright, sets neutral, and the calibrate LED then blinks slowly. A second press while relaxed records the relaxed posture instead of retaking neutral.
  - I rejected a dashboard button: the rider won't have a laptop mid-game, and it would need a protocol change.
  - I rejected locking the chair for 10 s after each press, because that breaks "no second press = behaves as now".
  - With a relaxed posture, speed stays at zero from upright to relaxed plus 0.15" (new setting 96), and forward reach is measured from the relaxed point. The posture is kept in RAM only.
  - A "relaxed" press more than 1.5" from neutral is treated as a lean and rejected.
  - In the replay, slumping now gives 0 speed instead of +0.14.
- **Automatic empty-seat zero, in RAM only.** It needs DISARM, an empty seat, and every channel steady within 12 counts for 3 s. It won't move a baseline more than 30 counts from the configured value, so a bag on the seat doesn't get zeroed out. Your reseated readings (383/396/420/404) are adopted, and a dashboard edit of a baseline wins over it.
  - The thresholds come from E9 empty-plate noise (σ about 1–2 counts) and are compile-time constants, so they cost no storage. Profile storage is now 7 bytes from full on the host build (15 on the Teensy).
- **Dashboard:** the four tiles move to the corners they measure (for example "Back-left (F)"), and a plate diagram shows the live CoP with a 2 s trail, neutral (+), relaxed posture (○), the no-output box and the full-lean box. The plus view, and firmware that doesn't report a layout, keep the old display.
  - The firmware sends a new 1 Hz `DBG CORNER` line with the box edges already computed, so the dashboard doesn't duplicate the math.
  - The new settings show up automatically through the existing schema-driven settings UI.
  - I checked it headless on the E9 replay at desktop and phone width and fixed one overlap of the corner tiles.
- **Mid-ride dead-channel check:** output stops and LEAN disarms if the CoP moves more than 0.75" between control ticks. From E9:
  - Healthy riding peaked at 0.54" per 50 ms (11.7 in/s), and getting out of the seat at 1.17" per 50 ms. At the slowest control period that's at most 0.36" per tick.
  - A dropped channel moves the CoP at once: F by at least 1.96", B at least 1.07", R at least 0.53" (1.06" in 95% of postures), L at least 0.22".
  - I also tried a diagonal-balance check (front-left + back-right against front-right + back-left), but healthy and dead readings overlap in E9, so I left it out.
- **Review item 5, done differently.** A short press does not reset the hold-calibrated reach. A hold calibration always ends in DISARM, and every LEAN entry needs a short press, so resetting on a short press would mean the hold reach could never be used. Instead it's dropped when a reach setting changes, when the seat has been empty for 3 s (a new rider), or when the profile changes.

## Files
- **Firmware:** `lean_layout.h/.cpp` (neutral zone, boxes, empty-plate tracker, new constants), `LeanDriveMode.*`, `main.cpp`, `DriveSettings.*`, `Protocol.cpp` (profile fix), the telemetry struct comment.
- **Tests:**
  - New `corner_calibration_test.cpp`.
  - Updated: `corner_layout_test`, `config_exhaustive_test`, `drive_settings_test`, the `Arduino.h` stub (an `analogRead` hook for the averaging test), and the plus golden test, regenerated from 8712aef.
- **Dashboard:** new `plate-view.js` with its tests; changes to `index.html`, `bluetooth.js`, `app.py`, `build-pages.cjs` and their tests.
- **Tools and docs:**
  - `chair_sql.py` (new `corner` table), `chair_summary.py`, `replay_cop.py`.
  - `CONFIGURATION.md`, `building-with-charles.md`, the dashboard README.
  - `experiments/README.md`: an E11 entry with your plate-on table, plus the replay results.

## Behaviour changes
- **Plus-layout steering is still bit-identical to 8712aef.** The golden test no longer hashes the rail flag, because:
- **The rail-fault threshold moved from 100 to 340 counts on both layouts, as intended.** A reading between 101 and 340 used to pass and now disarms LEAN.
- **Selecting a profile** now resets every field to its default before applying it, on both layouts. An old profile now gets defaults instead of inheriting the previous profile's values.
- **Corner layout only:**
  - Neutral calibration is rejected if any corner carries under 50 counts or under 5% of the load. LEAN stays stopped and the LED stays on.
  - The 0.75" CoP-jump disarm.
  - Minimum reach is now 1.2" (was 0.6").
  - The relaxed-posture press and the empty-seat zero.
- **Bluetooth transmit buffer is now 1024 bytes** (was 512). The worst 1 Hz burst is about 700 bytes, with room for your `DBG VESCPROBE`/`DBG VESCFW` lines. Expect a small merge in `main.cpp`'s 1 Hz block.
- **`DBG SENSORS`** now carries only `layout=`; the CoP, neutral and reach fields moved to `DBG CORNER`. Over USB/replay the dashboard console now hides `DBG` lines too, as Bluetooth already did.

## Open questions
1. **Collisions could trip the 0.75" jump disarm.** A hard chair-to-chair hit may move the CoP fast enough. It needs a bump test on the chair; `SENSOR_RAIL_DISARM` turns this check off along with the rail one.
2. **A channel dying while barely loaded isn't caught.** At back-touch, a dead L moves the CoP only 0.27" (R 0.62"); a test pins this down. The unplug-on-the-bench test will show what a pulled wire actually reads.
3. **With a relaxed posture, the default 2.0" forward reach is too long:** forward reaches only 0.50 speed. A hold calibration gives Mason 1.32" and full speed. Decide whether to change the default or require the hold.
4. **Collision risk with the plus-layout dynamic gains:** they still only reload on reboot or profile change. That predates this branch.
5. The relaxed posture only affects forward/back. Turning keeps the upright x and its 0.25" deadzone.

Charles sent the battery link. The site rate-limited me, so I'll retry it. The design doc already has what matters: **Bioenno 48 V 30 Ah lithium iron phosphate, charged by a 58.2 V / 4 A charger.** That means 16 cells in series. Mason read the motor as DB87-K3V53193, which is a Nanotec DB87.

**Correction on #2.** The −3 A regen limit is deliberate: the design doc caps regen at the battery's 4 A charge rating. Leave it. One catch: each VESC allows 3 A, so together they can push 6 A into the battery. Check the battery page for the BMS's real maximum charge current.

**A new problem from your battery info: overvoltage.**
- The VESC 6's hardware ceiling is **57 V**. It's in the VESC's own hardware file, and the setting is already at that cap. Your charger fills the pack to **58.2 V**.
- Straight off the charger, the pack is above what the VESCs accept. They throw an over-voltage fault and cut the motors.
- Regen braking on a nearly full pack pushes the voltage higher still, so the cut can happen *while you're braking*.
- These batteries settle to about 53–54 V after resting, so the danger window is the first stretch after a charge.
- Have you ever had the chair cut out right after charging? The fix is a charger that stops around 56 V, or a short drive before riding hard. The real hardware fix is a higher-voltage controller.
- Small thing for later: the VESCs think the battery is 15 lithium-ion cells. It's display-only, but I'll correct it when we write settings.

**Dashboard:** I agree with Mason that the VESC settings should be set-and-forget. I'll put a VESC panel on the dashboard:
- **Up front:** health (temperature, voltage, faults, frame age).
- **On a details tab:** the full settings both VESCs report.

**Charles's questions:**
- **How I got 0.16 m/s:** the firmware's own constant is 14.5454 (your gearbox and pulley) × 4 (motor pole pairs) = 58.2 motor ERPM per wheel RPM. The VESCs list the wheel as 0.61 m (24"). 300 ÷ 58.2 = 5.2 wheel RPM, which is about 0.16 m/s. **Confirm the wheel is 24"**; if it isn't, the speed scales with it.
- **Does "15 cells" break anything automatic?** No. It only feeds the VESC's battery-percentage estimate, and nothing on our chair reads that. The real cutoffs are set in volts (48/44 V), and those are right.
- **CDR currents:** the CDR only gives battery-side estimates: 12 A average, 42–44 A peak. It has no braking-current figure. Nothing has been measured while driving yet. That's the next item.

Mason, it did run, but **it wrote nothing.** Both VESCs still have their original settings; I confirmed by reading them back. The chair is exactly as it was.

**Why it failed:** the VESC rebuilds a long message from many small CAN frames, and if a single frame arrives out of order, it throws the whole message away. I was sending 8 frames at once, and the Teensy's CAN hardware doesn't guarantee they leave in order. **Fixed:** frames now go out one at a time, 2 ms apart, which takes about 150 ms per config.

I also made it easier to see what happens:
- It waits 30 s after boot, so the Bluetooth tap has reconnected before it starts. Last time I missed most of the step messages because the tap was still reconnecting.
- Every step reports how many frames it sent.
- After it finishes, it repeats the full result every 10 s.

**To retry:**
1. Plug USB back in. The upload starts by itself.
2. Unplug, battery on, chair disarmed.
3. Wait about 30 s.

It still only writes if each VESC matches the copy I reviewed byte for byte, then reads it back to confirm.

The line formats and byte budget are in the short report I sent earlier. The one figure to remember is about +2.8 kB/s, which takes the link to roughly 5.8 kB/s, about half of 115200 baud.

**Findings from the real log**
- **The 1 Hz burst was losing lines before this work.** The once-a-second lines went out together, about 700 B at once. In today's tap the last of them, `DBG VESC`, arrived intact only 82–88% of the time; the 20 Hz rows were about 99.6% intact. You can see truncated lines like `vin= 425 409`. Each 1 Hz line now has its own 20 Hz tick, so the busiest tick is about 515 B.
- **Fault polling is 10 Hz, not the 5 Hz in the brief.** A VESC holds its fault code for 500 ms (`m_fault_stop_time_ms`). Alternating at 5 Hz asks each VESC only every 400 ms, so one skipped poll would miss a fault. At 10 Hz each VESC is asked every 200 ms.
- **One reply at a time.** VESC reply frames don't say which VESC sent them, so two replies at once mix their bytes. A new monitor sends one GET at a time, the existing probe queues behind it, and nothing is asked while `vescApplier.active()`.
- **Things already in the log:**
  - The left VESC sent no status frames at all around 16:00.
  - The right VESC showed 4.9 V input.
  - The `right-app` settings write failed with "no reply" at 16:04.
  - The two motors' settings differ in a few places, for example `foc_openloop_rpm` is 700 left and 1400 right.

**Telemetry the chair didn't send before:**
- 20 Hz VESC currents and duty, plus the RPM smoother's internals.
- The VESC's RPM before the clamp to measured ± `MAX_RPM_COMMAND_DIFF`. The CSV only showed it after.
- Current peaks between 1 Hz lines.
- Events for every button, mode change, rail or CoP-jump disarm, calibration, profile and settings change.
- Loop and control-tick timing, CPU temperature, CAN error counters, and Bluetooth bytes and lines dropped.

**`main.cpp` merge areas:** the includes, `setMotorPower` (the speed cap moved to file scope), `activateProfile`, `updateModeInputs` and the new `logButtonEvents`, `applySeatedLatch`, `updateDrive` (event lines and tick timing only), `replyToConfiguration`, `loop()`, and `publishTelemetry`. Expect the conflict in `publishTelemetry`, which I rewrote around the 20 tick slots.

**What I couldn't measure:**
- **Nothing ran on the chair.** The bytes/s are worked out from the line formats, not measured on the radio. The new `DBG SYS` line (`bt=`, `drop=`, `btFree=`) will measure it on the first run.
- **`COMM_GET_VALUES` over CAN is untested on hardware.** It uses the same request and reply path the firmware-version probe already gets answers on. If it doesn't answer, `DBG VVAL n=asked/replies/lost` will show it.
- **No real status-frame bytes.** There's no raw CAN capture, so those tests build frames from the VESC 6.06 source. The settings decoders are tested on the real dumps from `current.csv`: in C++, and in JS field by field against `vesc_conf.py`.
- **The currents in `DBG VFAULT` are averages since the last poll, not the trip current.** For the left VESC over-current theory, the next step is the VESC's own fault log (terminal command `faults`), which records the current at the moment it tripped. That command is 7 bytes and needs the long CAN send plus a reply made of several text packets, so I left it out.
- **STATUS_5 isn't arriving yet.** Its age shows `-1` until `can_status_msgs_r2` is enabled on each VESC.
- **Motor temperature reads −270 °C.** That means no thermistor reading; the dashboard shows "no sensor reading".
- **Configuration reply bytes are approximate** (`cfg=` in `DBG SYS`).

Charles asked about the calibration routine. Do it in this order:

1. **Set your full-lean reach first.**
   - Put it in LEAN, sit upright, and **hold** the calibrate button.
   - While holding, lean all the way forward, back, left and right, then let go.
   - It ends in DISARM, as designed. Without this, full forward only gets about half speed once the relaxed posture is set.
2. **Set neutral.**
   - Go back into LEAN, sit upright and engaged, and **short press**.
   - The calibrate LED blinks slowly for 10 s.
3. **Set the relaxed posture.**
   - Within those 10 s, relax and **short press again**.
   - Slumping won't move the chair after that.
4. **Lean test, wheels off the ground (B1):**
   - Sitting upright and sitting relaxed should both give no motion.
   - Gentle forward should give a slow, smooth spin. Back should reverse. Left and right should turn.
   - Then stand up mid-lean (B2): the wheels should stop.

If a press gets rejected (the LED stays on), one corner is carrying almost no weight: sit back in the middle and press again. I'm watching the calibration lines and will tell you what neutral, relaxed and reach came out as.

I looked at the data from the motor give-out, the weak right turns and the slow S-turns. Fixes for two of them are ready to upload. The third needs live setting changes, so I've started building a way for me to make those changes myself.

**1. The motor "giving out" (17:08:13)**

This was the right VESC: it threw an **over-current fault**, released the motor for 0.6 s, then picked it up again. That's the 90–180° yaw Mason described, and the old "left motor gives out" was the same thing.

What happened when you stopped from full speed:
- The firmware tells each VESC "go 50 RPM slower than you are right now," over and over. At that gap the VESC's speed controller always asks for full braking.
- At speed, the battery's −3 A charge limit holds the braking back. That's why the chair rolls on for a while after you sit back.
- As the wheels slow, that limit stops mattering, so the braking current shoots up to the maximum. At the same time, the command follows the noisy low-speed RPM reading and jumps around.
- The current spiked past the right VESC's 52 A trip point, so the VESC shut the motor off.

**Fix now:** maximum braking current on both VESCs goes from 32.7 A to **20 A**. Braking at speed doesn't change, because the battery limit is what holds it there. At low speed the surge stays well under the trip point. You can still brake at about 2 m/s².

**Proper fix later:** replace the "50 RPM below measured" band-aid with a braking curve the VESC can actually follow.

**2. Right turns weaker than left**

You were right to suspect the calibration. Your right lean during calibration went further (2.7–2.9") than your left (2.3–2.4"). The chair wanted a bigger right lean for full turn, so right turns never got past 71%, while left hit full 53 times.

**Fix:** both turn directions now use the smaller of the two. Full output also now comes at 85% of your calibration lean, so full speed and full turn don't need a maximum lean.

**3. S-turns lag**

At speed, the wheels take **1.0–1.2 s** to follow a right-to-left switch; standing still it's 0.3 s. The speed smoother limits how fast acceleration can change, so after you switch it keeps pushing the old way for about half a second. That's the momentum throwing you. This is two settings (the jerk limit, 500 → ~1500, and the damping, 2 → ~1). I'll change them myself once my settings tool works.

**Upload:** fixes 1 and 2 are on (2543ddd) and upload by themselves when you **plug in USB with the battery off**. That batch also includes the jump-threshold and reverse-steering fixes. After you unplug and turn the battery on, wait about 30 s for the VESC braking change to write, then **redo the hold calibration**, because the new rules only apply to a fresh one.

**Plain-language tuning:** yes, let's do it that way. An agent is building a command-line tool so I can read and change the Teensy's settings myself, through the same tap. You tell me "it's twitchy turning left at speed," I check the data, change the number, and you ride again. Until it's done, ride and tell me what you feel. The S-turn settings are the first thing I'll change.

**I skimmed the council code, and two pieces line up with today's problems:**
1. **S-turns.** It smooths forward speed and turning *separately*. The old code smoothed each wheel on its own, so while the chair sped up or slowed down, the steering difference got squashed and came back late. That's very likely your S-turn lag.
2. **The motor give-out.** It replaces the "50 RPM below measured" band-aid. Stops ramp both wheels down together, and below the VESC's minimum speed it switches to direct brake current, which works down to standstill and can't reverse. That's exactly where today's current surge happened.

The calibrate button wasn't broken, and you're right about when the VESC writer should run. To your three questions:

**1. "The calibrate button seems finicky"**
- **It was rejecting your presses.** Mason sits far back, so the front-left sensor only carried about 75 of 2,050 counts. A new rule from the review demanded at least 5% on every corner (meant to catch a dead sensor), and it turned down your upright press about 10 times between 17:26 and 17:28.
- **Fixed (cad2952).** A dead sensor reads zero, so now any corner carrying at least 30 counts passes, and 75 is plenty.
- **The routine itself hasn't changed:** LEAN → hold calibrate and lean all four ways → it drops to DISARM → LEAN → short press while upright.
- **The new part is the blinking.** After the short press, the calibrate LED blinks slowly for 10 s. That's the window for an optional second "relaxed" press. You can ignore it and ride. Charles, you said earlier you'd rather just use active neutral and press DISARM to relax, so I'd remove that relaxed press and its blinking. I'm holding off because you said not to change too much at once. Say the word.

**2. "What were you fixing on the VESCs?"**
- **The motor give-out:** the right motor cutting out for half a second during a hard stop at 17:08.
- **The change:** VESC braking current from 32.7 A to **20 A**, so the low-speed braking surge can't hit the over-current trip. Braking at speed is unchanged.
- **Right now only the left has it.** You pressed LEAN while the writer was halfway through, and the writer stopped instead of finishing. The new build pauses and resumes instead.

**3. "Should buttons lock while the VESCs update? Never during regular play, right?"**
- **Right, it never runs during regular play.** It only writes when a VESC is missing an approved change. Once both have it, every power-on just reads, sees "already applied", and is done in about a second.
- **I'd rather not lock the buttons.** With the new build, pressing LEAN just pauses the writer. It finishes the next time you're in DISARM, and buttons never freeze on you.

**To finish:**
1. Plug in USB (battery off). The watcher uploads by itself, and **the four mode LEDs chase three times on boot**: that's your "upload worked" signal.
2. Unplug, battery on, and stay in DISARM for about 35 s so the right VESC gets its braking change. I'll confirm when both read 20 A.
3. Then calibrate as normal.

**Uploaded at 17:46:04.** The LEDs should have chased. That spiral happened on the *old* build, before the new turning limit went on. Ride this one before we decide anything.

What you're describing is real: **a feedback loop through your body.**
1. You snap left, the chair yaws left hard, and your upper body gets thrown right.
2. Your weight shifts right, which the chair reads as "turn right."
3. It turns right harder and throws you more, and that's the spiral.

How we can handle it, in order:
1. **On the chair now:** turn authority capped and fading with speed. That shrinks the loop, so the same body jerk produces less turn.
2. **If it still spirals:** limit how fast the turn command can *reverse* direction. Starting a turn stays snappy, but snapping from full right to full left gets about 0.2 s of smoothing, which takes the jolt out.
3. **Your "anticipate it" idea:** I can measure from the data how much your weight shifts per unit of chair rotation. Then the code subtracts that predicted body shift from the lean reading, which is real compensation. It needs a few logged S-turns to fit, so it's a next-session experiment, not something to rush onto the chair today.

Ride the new build: slow S-turns, then a right-to-left snap at walking pace. Tell me whether it still spirals.

**Settle these before anyone rides:**
1. **The −20 A braking limit is not in this build.** Today's `vesc_apply_data.h` patch (`l_current_min` −32.68 → −20 A) is written by the applier, and you asked for `VESC_APPLY_ENABLED = false` ([vesc_apply.h:22](<worktree>/wheelchair-firmware/src/vesc_apply.h:22)). Unless today's dev-setup build already ran on the chair after 17:15, the VESCs still brake at −32.68 A. Either upload dev-setup once first, or set the flag to `true` here. It only writes if the VESC holds exactly the expected "before" config.
2. **Turning at speed is much weaker than today.** The council caps sideways acceleration at 2.5 m/s². On speed knob 2, full forward plus full turn gives about 68°/s; today's turn mix gave about 245°/s (a wheel nearly stopped). Both figures assume the guessed 0.60 m track. The dashboard setting "Sideways acceleration limit" goes up to 6.

## How the conflicts were resolved
| Conflict | Resolution |
|---|---|
| Lean math | Today's `LeanDriveMode` and `lean_layout` are kept unchanged in their math. A new `command(travel, reverseLocked)` ([LeanDriveMode.cpp:156](<worktree>/wheelchair-firmware/src/driveModes/LeanDriveMode.cpp:156)) is the seam: it returns shaped speed and turn. Turn × `turnLimitRPM` becomes the rate ([DriveController.cpp:562](<worktree>/wheelchair-firmware/src/DriveController.cpp:562)). The turn-mix curve (ID 36) is gone. PLUS still works and is still selectable. |
| Reverse-turn flip | `lean::reverseTurn` ([lean_layout.cpp:183](<worktree>/wheelchair-firmware/src/driveModes/lean_layout.cpp:183)) keeps today's step flip at −0.1 and turn off below −0.9. On top sit the council's two rules: no flip while rolling forward above 15% of the limit, and the reverse lock. I dropped the council's fade of the flip itself: centred at −0.1 it cut the E9 "Right" hold (speed −0.06) to 40% turn. |
| CoP jump | New fault `COP_JUMP`, raised after `command()` ([DriveController.cpp:543](<worktree>/wheelchair-firmware/src/DriveController.cpp:543)). It disarms with a braking stop. |
| Calibration | Today's flow: upright press, optional relaxed press, hold-and-lean reach. The reach stays in RAM. The council's tare, range calibration and neutral trim are not in this build; the empty-seat zero replaces the tare. |
| Settings | Council IDs 14–17 and 52–56 (its ranges, sensor zeros, seat threshold) are not registered; I marked them never-reuse. Dropped: 1 (startup mode) and 36 (turn mix). Kept from the council: 37 turn rate (160), 38 sideways limit (2.5), 63 track width (default is a named, flagged `TRACK_WIDTH_ESTIMATE_M = 0.60`), 64 VESC minimum speed (15.5). Kept from dev-setup: 10–13, 50, 51 and 90–107. Deadband maximum now 0.5 (council bound). |
| Profile storage | 406 of 415 bytes used: 9 free on host, about 17 on Teensy. |
| Old profiles | Loading a profile resets everything to defaults, puts back the chair-owned fields, then applies the profile ([Protocol.cpp:162](<worktree>/wheelchair-firmware/src/config/Protocol.cpp:162)). So missing fields get defaults and nothing is inherited from the previous profile. Chair-owned means VESC IDs, wheel circumference, track width and VESC minimum speed. |
| `main.cpp` | Council's loop, watchdog and DriveController, plus today's slotted 1 Hz lines, VESC monitor, probe, config dump, all the `DBG` lines and the 1024-byte Bluetooth buffer. The council's `HEALTH` line now has its own slot (16). `DBG M` keeps its columns but now reports the new smoother: `pidL/R` = linear ± turn before the ±50 clamp. |
| VESC code | One `VescStatus`, from today's `vesc_decode.h`. `vesc_protocol.h` keeps the commands, brake current and `vescFresh`. |
| `Telemetry.cpp` | Council's "don't stall on USB" plus today's link counters. |
| Dashboard | `debugViewer/` is taken whole from dev-setup. The council's dashboard reads a `RANGES` line that no longer exists, so its changes are out. |
| README | The calibration section is rewritten for the corner flow; it described the council's tare and range calibration. |

## Tests and replay
- **Council's ride simulations:** all pass, randomized rides included. The test rig now places a rider's centre of pressure on the corner plate, using the council's lean units so its scenarios read the same.
  - The simulated body moves at most 2" per tick, because an instant lean trips the 2.5" jump fault.
  - Rewritten: the range-calibration test (now the reach hold), the tare test (now the empty-seat zero) and the 4-sample rail and 80 ms seat-bump checks (today's code reacts on one sample).
  - Removed: the neutral-trim test, and `lean_input_test` / `lean_calibration_test`, which test council math that isn't in this build.
- **Today's lean tests:** they run through a small shim. The E9 speed and turn numbers are unchanged; only the left/right wheel-share expectations changed, because the turn mix is gone.
- **Plus golden test:** it can't hold, since its hashes include the mixed wheel targets. It now checks speed and turn of the recorded samples against commit 8712aef, and those still match.
- **Replay:** `replay_cop.py` on E9/E10 gives identical speed and turn per hold versus dev-setup. Only the left/right columns differ, again from the missing turn mix. I made the tool's turn-mix lookup optional. The tool still flips at −0.3, not the firmware's −0.1; that was already the case.

## The speed clamp and the 17:08 overcurrent
The premise is off: the council did not replace `MAX_RPM_COMMAND_DIFF`. It still clamps to measured ± 50 RPM, in normal driving (turn gets the allowance first) and in the stop ramp ([DriveController.cpp:240](<worktree>/wheelchair-firmware/src/DriveController.cpp:240)). It adds `limitLead` and `unwind` on top.

What a stop does now:
- **Ramp:** from the lower of sent and measured speed, at 3.7 m/s² (117 RPM/s), with a 0.1 s soft start, down to 18 RPM (the VESC minimum-speed setting of 15.5 plus 2.5).
- **Below 18 RPM:** 15 A brake current on both wheels until under 3 RPM for 150 ms.
- **After that:** the chair is held; a moving wheel gets brake current of 1 A per RPM, between 3 and 15 A.
- **Normal driving below 18 RPM:** a wheel faster than its command gets that same proportional brake current instead of a speed command.

Does it avoid the fault? **Partly.** Below 18 RPM the VESC is told a fixed 15 A, not asked to chase a speed, and noise in the speed reading stops mattering there. Above 18 RPM, a regen-limited wheel that can't keep up with the ramp still gets a command at measured − 50, so the VESC still demands full braking, exactly as today. At kp 0.004, about 4 RPM of error already saturates it. The −20 A VESC limit is the real cap, and see item 1 above.

I left the VESC minimum speed at 15.5, though the chair's VESCs are at 300 ERPM (≈5.2 RPM). That keeps the fixed 15 A brake handling the stop from 18 RPM down, instead of from about 7.7 RPM.

## What changes on the chair versus today's dev-setup
- **Stops:**
  - DISARM, an empty seat, a fault or the remote's stop button now brake the chair to a stop. Today they cut current and it coasts.
  - The empty seat triggers on a single sample.
  - After a fault the chair stays held until DISARM is pressed.
- **Holding:** a disarmed chair with nobody in it is held. Tap DISARM to free-wheel it for pushing by hand. With a rider in DISARM, or in Assist, it is free.
- **Turning:**
  - On the spot it's 160°/s on every knob; today it was about 158°/s on knob 2 and scaled with the knob.
  - At speed it's capped by the sideways limit (see item 2).
  - S-turn reversal on knob 2: the turn command crosses zero in 0.03 s (today 0.46 s) and reaches 90% of the new turn in 0.32 s (today 0.87 s). Simulated on the real smoother code.
  - The council's smoother also stops the old "keeps accelerating the wrong way" behaviour of the forward axis.
- **Reverse:** half the knob speed. A back lean that starts while rolling forward only brakes; the rider has to return to neutral before reversing.
- **Speed caps:** the knob only (25/50/75/100% of 4.92 m/s). Nothing extra was added for first rides.
- **Remote:**
  - Its stop button now works in LEAN and Assist too.
  - The stick must be centred once before it drives.
  - It obeys the same reverse and turn limits as LEAN.
  - Its timeout can't be set above 200 ms.
  - It keeps driving with an empty seat.
- **Assist:** the council's version. Boost only follows a push that already sped the wheel up, with a 100 ms wait between boosts. It can't start or keep the chair moving on its own.
- **Calibration press:** ignored above 20 RPM.
- **Power-on:** always DISARM, and the chair brakes itself if it's rolling at power-on.

## The council's three claims, checked
- **Watchdog:** it does not brake during a freeze. It resets the Teensy after 0.5 s. Until then the VESCs' own 250 ms timeout and 5 A brake decide what happens.
- **Open sensor wire:** not detected directly. An open sensor reads as unloaded (≈372), which is above the 340 rail. It's only caught if the centre of pressure jumps more than 2.5" in one tick, or at calibration (a corner under 50 counts is rejected).
- **Dashboard editing:** the new settings are ordinary settings, so the generic settings panel can list them. Writes are only accepted in DISARM with the chair stopped, never during a ride. I didn't check the panel itself.

## Open questions (conservative defaults kept)
- **Track width:** 0.60 m is a guess. Measure it; if the real track is wider, turns come out slower than the setting.
- **Turn rate:** 160°/s kept, not the spec's 200.
- **Sideways limit:** 2.5 m/s². Expect "turns feel weak at speed".
- **VESC minimum speed:** setting is 15.5, the chair's VESCs are at about 5.2 (reasoning above).
- **Empty-chair hold:** on. **REMOTE with an empty seat:** allowed.
- **Speed cap for first rides:** none beyond the knob. Start on knob 1–2.
- **Applier:** whether to turn it on to get the −20 A limit (item 1).
- **Seat and rail:** no 80 ms filter on the empty seat; a bump disarms with a braking stop. The rail fault also triggers on one sample.

The new test covers:
- **Side leans:** pure E9 Right and Left holds don't engage the latch. Raw speed is -0.162, below the engage threshold, but the shaped speed is -0.06, and the turn keeps its direction.
- **Engage:** it engages once shaped speed drops below -0.15.
- **Hover and standstill:** it stays flipped while hovering around the threshold and near standstill while still turning.
- **Release:** it releases only when shaped speed is above -0.02 and shaped turn is below 0.15.

Moving between postures ramps through intermediate ones, and a ramp that passes through standstill with a small turn is a real release. The "straight back, no release" step therefore goes via a deep back lean first.

2. **Steady speeds:** 15, 30, 60 and 90 RPM, forward and reverse. It measures how much current each side needs just to spin. **A side that needs noticeably more current, or whose current is rough, points to binding or misalignment**, which fits the quick-release problem.
3. **Step response:** 0 → 60 → 0 RPM. How fast and cleanly each side follows a command.
4. **Braking:** 90 RPM to stop on brake current. Stopping time per side.

- I start it from the laptop.
- It only runs in DISARM with an empty seat, both VESCs healthy and the wheels still.

**Claude**:
Yes, I like it, with one caveat that changes how far you can trust the results.

**What we've recorded**
- **Today 16:26–18:33** (most of the court session), at 20 times a second:
  - the raw value of all four sensors;
  - the lean point and the speed/turn command;
  - each wheel's target, smoothed and actual RPM;
  - motor current and duty, plus the speed smoother's internals.
- **Events:** every button press, mode change, calibration press and the result, the calibration values once a second, VESC faults, and battery voltage.
- **Last night's** experiment runs (sensors and the lean commands of that time).
- **Gaps:**
  - about 5–15% of lines got garbled or dropped over Bluetooth;
  - about 36 s lost when the tap crashed;
  - the e-stop restarts;
  - nothing before 16:26 today.
  
  It's enough to replay most of the ride.

**Two ways to use it**

**1. Simulation on the laptop (my first choice).**
- Feed the recorded sensor stream and button events into each firmware version compiled for the laptop, and compare what each one tells the wheels, moment by moment.
- The tests already run the lean math this way. The council branch even has a ride simulator with a simple model of the chair.
- You'd get numbers like:
  - "on Mason's 17:01 S-turn, today's build reverses the wheel difference in 1.0 s and council's in 0.1 s";
  - "on the 17:39 spin input, today's build sends a wheel to −155 RPM and council's caps it at −58".
- **Cheap, repeatable, no hardware.** It's ideal for comparing versions, or the same version with different settings.
- Add a simple model of the chair (mass, turning inertia, the −3 A braking cap, the track width you still need to measure) and it also estimates how the chair would actually *move*.

**2. Replaying it on the chair with the wheels off.**
- The Teensy plays back the recorded sensors instead of reading the real ones, and the real VESCs and motors respond.
- It adds what simulation can't: real VESC behaviour, current spikes and faults.
- It's weak on everything else. With no chair weight and no ground, a free-spinning motor speeds up, brakes and draws current nothing like it does under load, and the braking cap that caused today's problems barely shows. It's good for "does this version ever ask for something the VESCs fault on", and poor for "what will it feel like".

**The caveat that matters**
A replay is **open loop**. Mason's leans in the log were reactions to *today's* code. On the council build he'd have leaned differently, because the chair would have moved differently. So a replay tells you **how each version responds to the same input**, which is a fair comparison of the code, but not how a ride on it would go. The death spiral in particular is a loop through the rider's body, and a replay can't reproduce it. Only a simulation that includes a simple model of the body in the loop, or a real ride, can show it.

**How I'd do it**
1. Build the laptop replay harness that runs both builds over today's sessions. Put out side-by-side charts plus a scorecard:
   - S-turn reversal time;
   - top wheel speeds;
   - spin rate;
   - braking profile;
   - any moment one wheel is commanded backwards while still rolling forward.
2. Add the chair model once you've measured track width and roughly weighed chair plus rider.
3. Use the wheels-off replay only as a safety screen before a new build's first ride: run the scariest recorded moments and check nothing faults.

On the settings: the two builds share most of them (reach, deadzone, smoother), so I'd run both with profile 1's values. Where they differ (council's turn rate and sideways limit versus today's turn authority), the scorecard shows that difference on purpose.

The bench test is built and committed on `charles/bench-test`, in two commits on top of `charles/dev-setup` (f08f8c2). Nothing is pushed or uploaded. It has not run on the chair, only against a simulated pair of motors. `./run-tests.sh` passes (native, tools, dashboard JS and Python) and a clean `pio run` builds with no warnings.

**Commits:** `b79c07c` (firmware routine, RAM-only field, tests), then `945a394` (report tool, VESC-write lockout, CAL LED, docs). Review the head, `945a394`.

**Trigger** (wheels off, nobody in the chair, DISARM):
```bash
python.exe tools/chair_config.py set "bench" 1     # "bench" 0 aborts
python3 tools/bench_report.py                      # afterwards: table + chart
```

**Steps** (about 65 s; both wheels always get the same command):

| Steps | What | Time |
|---|---|---|
| `creep3`, `creep5`, `creep8` | hold 3, 5, 8 RPM | 3 s each |
| `fwd15` … `fwd90` | hold 15, 30, 60, 90 RPM | 5 s each, stats over the last 2 s |
| `stopF` | 8 A brake until still | timeout 6 s |
| `rev15` … `rev90` | same speeds in reverse | 5 s each |
| `stopR` | brake until still | timeout 6 s |
| `up1`, `down1`, `stop1`, `up2`, `down2`, `stop2` | 0→60, 60→0, twice; time to 90%, overshoot, settling | 3 s per step |
| `spin90` | spin to 90 RPM, hold 1 s | timeout 6 s |
| `brake` | 15 A brake; time to < 3 RPM per side | timeout 6 s |
| — | release (zero current), field back to 0 | |

I made the steady holds 5 s instead of 4 s: at 4 s the total came out at about 55 s, under your 60–90 s target. It also gives each speed 3 s to settle before the stats window.

**Start gates:** any failure logs `DBG BENCH ... refused why=…` and sets the field back to 0.
- Mode is DISARM (so never LEAN, REMOTE or ASSIST).
- Seat empty. Tonight's real log reads `seated=0` on the empty plate, with plenty of margin.
- No button held.
- Safety brake not latched. **I added this one.** If Charles climbed out while in LEAN, the DISARM LED is blinking and he must press DISARM first.
- No VESC settings write running.
- Both VESCs reported speed within the last 200 ms and show no fault code.
- Both wheels under 3 RPM.

**Aborts:**
- What it does: 15 A brake until both wheels are under 3 RPM (at most 4 s), then zero current, logged with `abort why=` and `released why=`.
- Triggers:
  - any button, held or pressed;
  - the seat becoming occupied;
  - a VESC silent for 200 ms;
  - a fault code, or a new fault that came and went between polls;
  - the field set to 0;
  - a step timing out;
  - the safety brake latching;
  - a VESC settings write starting. That write is also blocked from starting during a run.
- If the mode leaves DISARM, it stops commanding at once and doesn't brake, because the new mode owns the motors.
- The CAL LED blinks fast while it runs.

**How the field stays out of EEPROM:** it is field 110, OFF/RUN, and I added a RAM-only flag for it to the config framework.
- Every save leaves it out: EEPROM, profiles and the unsaved-changes flag.
- Loading ignores it if it ever shows up in stored data, so it is OFF at power-on.
- The dashboard's profile save sends every field, so the firmware drops this one from the upload before writing.
- Revert, restore-defaults and switching profiles all reset it to OFF.
- A change to only this field no longer starts the 2 s auto-save.
- It costs 0 profile bytes. A dashboard upload that includes it still fits; a test checks that.

**Logging:**
- Each step logs a start line, a summary and an end line, all starting with `DBG BENCH`.
- The summary has per side: mean and sd of motor current and RPM, whether the setpoint was reached, and for steps the time to 90%, overshoot and settling. The brake step reports the time to stop.
- These lines are queued and only sent when Bluetooth has room, so a summary can't be dropped.
- `tools/bench_report.py` prints a left-vs-right table and flags current asymmetry over 25% (skipped when both sides are under 0.5 A), ripple over 10% of the setpoint, and missed setpoints. It also writes `experiments/bench-<log>-run<N>.html` (RPM and current, left vs right on the same axes).
- Its tests run the real firmware routine through a small C++ program, so the line format and the report can't drift apart.

**Things to know before uploading:**
- `creep3` is below the VESC's minimum speed (≈5.2 RPM), so expect "not reached" there. The report marks it as expected.
- An existing quirk, not touched: `vescSetWheelRPM` takes a whole-number RPM, so `setMotorPower`'s fractional RPM commands get truncated in normal driving. The bench uses whole-number setpoints, so it isn't affected.
- Stats are sampled every 5 ms but the VESCs report every 20 ms, so each value repeats about 4 times. The means are fine; the sample count `n` is inflated.
- After a run ends, the dashboard keeps showing RUN until it re-reads the settings. `chair_config.py get "bench"` shows the real value.
- **This upload commands the motors**, so it needs the usual check-in before going on the chair.

Docs are in `building-with-charles.md` under "Wheels-off bench test".

Since the wheels came off, the chair hasn't been on battery once in my logs.
- **Last time the battery was on: 18:20:45** at the court, 53.4 V on both VESCs.
- **After that, only USB-powered sessions (21:16–21:39).** On those, everything I can see worked:
  - the Teensy booted;
  - all four seat sensors read normally;
  - Bluetooth connected;
  - the right VESC answered over CAN (it gets USB power through its 5 V pin, as Mason explained).
  
  So the Teensy, the sensors, the Bluetooth module and the CAN wiring are fine.
- **Right now I see nothing.** No Teensy on USB and no Bluetooth link, so no power is reaching the board at all.

**That puts the problem on the battery side.** The board gets its 5 V *from the right VESC*, which only has power when the battery reaches it. Nothing lighting up means battery power isn't getting to the VESCs.

**What I'd check, most likely first:**
1. **The e-stop / main breaker.** In the design doc the e-stop *is* the main breaker between the battery and everything else. Make sure it's reset (twisted or pulled out, or the breaker flipped back on). You hit it a lot today.
2. **The main battery connector** under the seat, between the battery and the chair's harness. All that hammering could have pulled it half out.
3. **The VESC power leads and anything routed near the wheels or axles.** Look for a pulled or pinched wire, especially on the side that fought you most.
4. **The battery itself.** Put a multimeter on its output: it should read about 53 V. If it reads 0, the battery's protection circuit has probably shut it off. Plugging in the charger for a moment usually wakes it.
5. **Any inline fuse** on the battery lead.

**How I can help while you check:** with **USB unplugged and the battery switched on**, watch whether the Bluetooth module's LED lights or blinks. If it does, the chair has power and I'll see it connect within seconds: I'll read both VESC voltages and tell you exactly which side is live. The tap is running and watching.

2. **Key Technical Concepts**
   - **Hardware and firmware stack:**
     - Teensy 4.1 firmware (PlatformIO; upload from WSL via `wsl_upload.py` and the Windows Teensy tools).
     - HC-06 Bluetooth on COM4; one connection at a time.
     - Two TRAMPA VESC 6 MkVI controllers, firmware 6.06, hw 60_MK6, CAN IDs left 2 / right 26, extended CAN frames at 500 kbps.
   - **Corner layout.** Sensor channel → corner: R = front-right, L = front-left, F = back-left, B = back-right. Positions in inches from the old plus centre: FL(−5.75, 3.0), FR(5.375, 2.9375), BL(−5.75, −4.1875), BR(5.3125, −4.25). Amp zero is about 372. The empty plate reads about 391/406/427/400.
   - **VESC over CAN:**
     - COMM packets via CAN_PACKET_PROCESS_SHORT_BUFFER(8), FILL_RX_BUFFER(5), FILL_RX_BUFFER_LONG(6), PROCESS_RX_BUFFER(7) with CRC16 (XMODEM, poly 0x1021).
     - VESC 6.06 drops fill frames that arrive out of order, so they must be sent one at a time.
     - mcconf is 483 bytes, appconf 278 bytes; decoded from the `confgenerator_6_06.c` layout.
     - `foc_offsets_current/voltage` are re-measured at every boot, so they're volatile bytes.
   - **VESC 6.06 behaviour:**
     - `s_pid_min_erpm`: a setpoint below it gives duty 0 (brake), and a stopped motor won't start below it.
     - Speed PID kp 0.004 saturates beyond about 4 RPM of error.
     - The battery regen limit `l_in_current_min` = −3 A caps braking at speed.
     - The hardware input-voltage limit is 57 V; the charger output is 58.2 V.
     - Fault codes: 1 = OVER_VOLTAGE, 2 = UNDER_VOLTAGE, 3 = DRV, 4 = ABS_OVER_CURRENT.
   - **Battery:** Bioenno 48 V 30 Ah LiFePO4, 16S; charger 58.2 V / 4 A; 30 A continuous / 60 A for 30 s.
   - **Drive mechanics:**
     - wheel 24" (0.61 m); 58.18 ERPM per wheel RPM (gear 14.5454 × 4 pole pairs);
     - `MAX_RPM_COMMAND_DIFF` = 50 clamp to the measured RPM;
     - RPMSetpointPID smoother with jerk limit and kd damping;
     - turn mix; reverse-turn flip.
   - **Body-in-the-loop "death spiral":** yaw jerk shifts the rider's centre of pressure.
   - **Config protocol:** COBS-framed; settings change only in DISARM; profiles are 7 slots selected by a knob and reloaded at power-on (this overrode main-bank saves); snapshot TLVs.
   - **Tooling:**
     - live skill (Whisper final pass, `wait-final`, `live summary`);
     - T3 `delegate_task` agents (Opus 5.5 / Fable 5.2 / Sonnet 5.5);
     - tap WebSocket bridge (stdlib).

   - **Fable review findings:**

   - **VESC write #1 wrote nothing:** out-of-order fill frames. Fixed with one frame per 2 ms.
   - **Write #2:** the motor jobs aborted on the boot-measured foc_offsets. Fixed by patching onto the live config while ignoring the volatile bytes.
   - **Write #3:** left written, right aborted because the chair entered LEAN. Fixed with pause/resume; the right was written at 17:35:58.
   - **Mislabel:** I first called fault 3 "under-voltage"; it's DRV.
   - **Bad battery claims:** I suggested raising regen; corrected, since −3 A is deliberate per the CDR. The overvoltage risk was overstated; Charles noted it was knowledge, not measurement. STATUS_5 now measures 53.4–53.6 V.

   - **Over-current:** right ABS_OVER_CURRENT at 17:08:13 and left at 18:03:37, from the brake surge and reverse-plugging. Braking current −20 A, the mean clamp, and the no-plug fixes.
   - **Death spiral:**
     - from the turn mix growing with speed, so a reverse full side lean sent a wheel to full reverse;
     - fixed with turn authority, the latch, then flip off.
   - **Latch on raw speed:** a pure side lean (raw −0.162) engaged the latch and turned the wrong way. Moved to shaped speed, then the flip was disabled.
   - **Calibration rejections:** the 5% share rule, then the 30-count floor, rejected light corners. Now only raw < 382 is rejected.
   - **Lost settings:** saved settings were lost at power-on because the profile reloaded. Fixed with auto-save into the active profile.
